Mathematical framework:
The certified set is C_B = {x | B(x) >= 0}. It must stay inside X and be forward invariant. States outside X are unsafe.
No nontrivial seed set is required.
B(x) < 0 for every unsafe state in the query.
On {x in X | B(x) = 0}, require the strict boundary condition L_f B > 0. All flow checks are only on X.
All certificate checks use the closed-loop dynamics after substituting the given controller. The given controller must satisfy pi(x) in U on X.

Objective:
Propose a C1 barrier whose super-level set is forward invariant and separated from the unsafe set. Do not alter or invent a seed set. Among candidates satisfying every required condition, make the volume of the certified invariant set as large as possible. Never write verified, proved, certified, UNSAT, or globally valid unless that result is already in the supplied evidence. Jointly design the required controller inside the declared control set U. Among formally valid controllers, prefer a structurally simple one with modest magnitude on X. Try a constant, linear, or affine controller first; use a nonlinear controller only if those simple forms cannot satisfy the formal conditions.

Certificate-family discipline:
Choose the simplest certificate structure justified by the dynamics, domain, and verification conditions. Non-polynomial dynamics do not by themselves require a non-polynomial certificate. Use transcendental or rational terms only when construction_summary gives a concrete identity or inequality showing how they help close the required condition. Ensure every expression is well-defined on X. Structure examples only. Polynomial: for dx1/dt = -x1 + x1*x2 and dx2/dt = -x2 on x1**2 + x2**2 <= 1, V = x1**2 + x2**2 gives D V = -2*x1**2*(1-x2) - 2*x2**2 <= 0. Non-polynomial: for dx1/dt = -x1*(1+x1**2), V = log(1+x1**2) gives the exact identity D V = -2*x1**2, with 1+x1**2 > 0.

Constraints:
Use only declared state variables (x1, x2, x3, x4, x5, x6). Do not leave free symbols such as delta or epsilon. Every controller assignment must obey the Controller observations information pattern shown above; the pipeline rejects the candidate after parsing if an assignment reads any other state. Return certificate and controller expressions as concise strings; never emit recursive AST nodes. Numeric literals are parsed as exact rationals: 0.01 becomes 1/100, and 1/100 is also fine. The name pi denotes the exact constant Real.pi; do not replace it by a decimal approximation. Allowed function names: sin,cos,exp,tanh,log,sqrt,atan,ln,tan,sec,csc,cot,sinh,cosh,sech,csch,coth,arctan,atanh. log is the natural logarithm. sqrt(arg) requires arg >= 0 on X; atan is the two-sided inverse tangent; atanh(arg) may be used only when -1 < arg < 1 on X. Names other than sin,cos,exp,tanh,log,sqrt,atan are expanded to the stored AST before verification; for example sech(x1) becomes 2/(exp(x1)+exp(-x1)). The equivalent square-root spellings sqrt(x), x**(1/2), and x**0.5 are all accepted and normalized to the same exact AST. Division of expressions is allowed, for example x1**2/(1+x1**4); write parentheses when the denominator is a sum. Do not invent other function names and do not use Abs. The pipeline symbolically differentiates B and independently checks the continuous-time queries. construction_summary is a construction note, not a certificate; downstream Lean proof agents read it as their roadmap, so it must name the useful auxiliary quantities, give a factorised form of the Lie expression grad B dot f_cl in those quantities, and list the boundedness facts with explicit constants that close the sign inequality.

Constraints:
Do not change definitions, theorem statement, system, controller, candidate, or candidate hash. Do not emit a top-level lemma inside proof_body; that text is inserted only after :=. No sorry, admit, axiom, external trusted assumption, full project, Markdown, or natural-language proof sketch. If you choose helpers, put one declaration in each helper_lemmas entry in dependency order and keep each helper focused. Keep one-use facts as local have/calc steps. If compiler_diagnostics lists errors, fix all of them in this one reply; do not wait for a later round. State n is Fin n → ℝ. RadiallyUnbounded uses R ≤ ∑ i, (x i)^2, not ‖x‖ or EuclideanSpace.norm_sq_eq. That radius is not a claim that V is quadratic. The locked definitions are ordinary Lean expressions, not DSL ASTs. Use the generated barrier_unfold_* and closedLoop_apply_* simp lemmas when exposing their formulas. nlinarith and linarith only close polynomial or linear goals; they cannot see Real.exp, Real.log, or Real.tanh, but they treat such subterms as atoms, so first derive sign facts about them with have and pass those facts in. The locked statement already has (hV : ContDiff ℝ 1 barrier); use hV, do not re-prove smoothness with decide or isTotalSmooth. Use only lemma names that exist in Mathlib or that you fully prove in helper_lemmas; never cite invented names such as lean_barrier_*. Before the expensive proof compile, the harness prechecks qualified Real.* names; this is only an early name check, and declarations from any Lean or Mathlib namespace remain allowed when the normal compiler accepts them.

Recurring pitfalls:
The locked statement already binds every hypothesis such as (hV : ContDiff ℝ 1 barrier); the goal starts at the ¬ ∃. Begin the proof with rintro ⟨x, hx⟩ (or intro h), never with intro hV. For a fixed-parameter condition the locked counterexample statement also binds one or more scalar parameters after x. Destructure all existential binders, retain the exact interval hypotheses, and prove the inequality uniformly; the generated barrier still has type State n → ℝ and therefore must not be redefined as a function of those parameters. Cite only Mathlib lemma names you are certain exist; when a name fails with unknown constant, do not guess variant names on the next round. Treat the proposition intended by that name as an explicit subgoal: prove it from verified declarations as a local have, or as a named helper only when it is genuinely reusable, then use that fact in the main proof. The compiler feedback includes a local Mathlib source search for the failed name: use only declarations listed there as ingredients. Close intermediate have steps with linarith [lemma] or nlinarith [lemma] rather than exact lemma: exact demands a syntactically identical type and fails on argument order or associativity differences that linarith absorbs. When a Type mismatch shows two types that differ only by parenthesisation or term order, do not restructure the proof; run ring_nf at h ⊢ first or replace exact h with linarith [h]. For high-dimensional systems, prefer vector, matrix, finite-sum, quadratic-form, and operator-norm arguments. In coupled finite-dimensional systems, keep coordinates as functions on Fin n and prove bounds on ∑ i, (x i)^2 with tools such as Finset.sum_le_sum, Cauchy--Schwarz, and Young inequalities. Do not expand every coordinate square into one giant ring_nf goal; connect generated coordinate definitions to the abstract vector or matrix expression with a small extensional bridge lemma.

The synthesis agent wrote the construction note below for this exact candidate; it usually names the intended variable decomposition, the factorised form of the derivative expression, and the boundedness facts that make the decay argument work. The locked statement is the fully expanded form of that expression: first recover the note's decomposition with let/set, then prove along the note's argument.
Construction note: Let h=981/100-x2-x5, y1=x1, y2=x4, y3=h*tan(x3), y4=x2*tan(x3)+h*(1+tan(x3)^2)*x6, and r0=y1, r1=y1+y2, r2=y1+2*y2+y3, r3=y1+3*y2+3*y3+y4. Write Q=x2^2+x2*x5+x5^2, V0=Q+sum(r_i^2), G=(1/50)((5*x3)^20+(5*x6)^20), and B=1/50-V0-G. The controller gives dot(x5)=-x2-x5, dot(y1)=y2, dot(y2)=y3, dot(y3)=y4, and dot(y4)=-y1-4*y2-6*y3-4*y4. Hence dot(V0)=-D, where D=Q+(r0-r1)^2+(r1-r2)^2+(r2-r3)^2+r0^2+r3^2, and L_f B=D-2*((5*x3)^19*x6+(5*x6)^19*u2). Also D>=3*V0/8. On X, h is in [941/100,1021/100], cos(x3)>=49/50, |tan(x3)|<=1/4, and 1+tan(x3)^2<=17/16; these give |u1|<1 and |u2|<3. On B=0, V0<=1/50 implies |5*x3|<=1/5 and |5*x6|<=2/5. Thus V0>(999/1000)/50, D>1/1000, and the absolute guard contribution is below 1/1000000, so L_f B>0. Separation follows from V0>=x1^2, V0>=3*x2^2/4, V0>=3*x5^2/4, V0>=x4^2/2, while G>1/50 whenever |x3|>1/5 or |x6|>1/5.

No sorry, admit, axiom, external trusted assumption, full project, Markdown, or natural-language proof sketch. Use the generated barrier_unfold_* and closedLoop_apply_* simp lemmas when exposing their formulas. nlinarith and linarith only close polynomial or linear goals; they cannot see Real.exp, Real.log, or Real.tanh, but they treat such subterms as atoms, so first derive sign facts about them with have and pass those facts in. The locked statement already has (hV : ContDiff ℝ 1 barrier); use hV, do not re-prove smoothness with decide or isTotalSmooth. Use only lemma names that exist in Mathlib or that you fully prove in helper_lemmas; never cite invented names such as lean_barrier_*. Before the expensive proof compile, the harness prechecks qualified Real.* names; this is only an early name check, and declarations from any Lean or Mathlib namespace remain allowed when the normal compiler accepts them.

Recurring pitfalls:
The locked statement already binds every hypothesis such as (hV : ContDiff ℝ 1 barrier); the goal starts at the ¬ ∃. Begin the proof with rintro ⟨x, hx⟩ (or intro h), never with intro hV. For a fixed-parameter condition the locked counterexample statement also binds one or more scalar parameters after x. Destructure all existential binders, retain the exact interval hypotheses, and prove the inequality uniformly; the generated barrier still has type State n → ℝ and therefore must not be redefined as a function of those parameters. Cite only Mathlib lemma names you are certain exist; when a name fails with unknown constant, do not guess variant names on the next round. Treat the proposition intended by that name as an explicit subgoal: prove it from verified declarations as a local have, or as a named helper only when it is genuinely reusable, then use that fact in the main proof. The compiler feedback includes a local Mathlib source search for the failed name: use only declarations listed there as ingredients. Close intermediate have steps with linarith [lemma] or nlinarith [lemma] rather than exact lemma: exact demands a syntactically identical type and fails on argument order or associativity differences that linarith absorbs. When a Type mismatch shows two types that differ only by parenthesisation or term order, do not restructure the proof; run ring_nf at h ⊢ first or replace exact h with linarith [h]. For high-dimensional systems, prefer vector, matrix, finite-sum, quadratic-form, and operator-norm arguments. In coupled finite-dimensional systems, keep coordinates as functions on Fin n and prove bounds on ∑ i, (x i)^2 with tools such as Finset.sum_le_sum, Cauchy--Schwarz, and Young inequalities. Do not expand every coordinate square into one giant ring_nf goal; connect generated coordinate definitions to the abstract vector or matrix expression with a small extensional bridge lemma.

Author's proof roadmap:
The synthesis agent wrote the construction note below for this exact candidate; it usually names the intended variable decomposition, the factorised form of the derivative expression, and the boundedness facts that make the decay argument work. The locked statement is the fully expanded form of that expression: first recover the note's decomposition with let/set, then prove along the note's argument.
Construction note: Let h=981/100-x2-x5, y0=x1, y1=x4, y2=h*tan(x3), y3=x2*tan(x3)+h*(1+tan(x3)^2)*x6, and r0=y0, r1=y0+y1, r2=y0+2*y1+y2, r3=y0+3*y1+3*y2+y3. Put Q=x2^2+x2*x5+x5^2, V=Q+r0^2+r1^2+r2^2+r3^2, H=(1/100)((5*x3)^10+(5*x6)^10), so B=1/100-V-H. Closed loop gives dot(x5)=-x2-x5, dot(r0)=r1-r0, dot(r1)=r2-r1, dot(r2)=r3-r2, dot(r3)=-r3. Thus dot(V)=-D, where D=Q+(r0-r1)^2+(r1-r2)^2+(r2-r3)^2+r0^2+r3^2, and L_f B=D-(1/2)*((5*x3)^9*x6+(5*x6)^9*u2), with D>=3*V/8. On X, h in [941/100,1021/100], cos(x3)>=49/50, |tan(x3)|<=1/4, and 1+tan(x3)^2<=17/16; hence |u1|<1 and |u2|<3. If B=0, V<=1/100, y2=r2-2*r1+r0 gives |x3|<=40/941<1/20, and y3=r3-3*r2+3*r1-r0 gives |x6|<9/100. Therefore |5*x3|<1/4, |5*x6|<9/20, H<(1/100000), V>999/100000, D>2997/800000, while the guard term has magnitude below 3/2500; hence L_f B>0. Separation uses V>=x1^2, V>=x4^2/2, Q>=(x2^2+x5^2)/2, and H>1/100 if |x3|>1/5 or |x6|>1/5.

For a fixed-parameter condition the locked counterexample statement also binds one or more scalar parameters after x. Destructure all existential binders, retain the exact interval hypotheses, and prove the inequality uniformly; the generated barrier still has type State n → ℝ and therefore must not be redefined as a function of those parameters. Cite only Mathlib lemma names you are certain exist; when a name fails with unknown constant, do not guess variant names on the next round. Treat the proposition intended by that name as an explicit subgoal: prove it from verified declarations as a local have, or as a named helper only when it is genuinely reusable, then use that fact in the main proof. The compiler feedback includes a local Mathlib source search for the failed name: use only declarations listed there as ingredients. Close intermediate have steps with linarith [lemma] or nlinarith [lemma] rather than exact lemma: exact demands a syntactically identical type and fails on argument order or associativity differences that linarith absorbs. When a Type mismatch shows two types that differ only by parenthesisation or term order, do not restructure the proof; run ring_nf at h ⊢ first or replace exact h with linarith [h]. For high-dimensional systems, prefer vector, matrix, finite-sum, quadratic-form, and operator-norm arguments. In coupled finite-dimensional systems, keep coordinates as functions on Fin n and prove bounds on ∑ i, (x i)^2 with tools such as Finset.sum_le_sum, Cauchy--Schwarz, and Young inequalities. Do not expand every coordinate square into one giant ring_nf goal; connect generated coordinate definitions to the abstract vector or matrix expression with a small extensional bridge lemma.
